    {
      "id": "Hamsa adalah burung air dalam teks Sanskerta kuno yang menjadi simbol rohani budaya India dan Asia Tenggara. Para sarjana menafsirkan burung acuannya sebagai angsa, swan, atau flamingo.",
      "en": "Hamsa is an aquatic bird in ancient Sanskrit texts serving as a spiritual symbol in India and Southeast Asia. Scholars interpret its referent as goose, swan, or flamingo.",

        "hamsa-bird-c01",
        "hamsa-bird-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan kemampuan mitos memisahkan susu dari campuran susu dan air. Dalam ikonografi ia menjadi kendaraan Brahma, Gayatri, Saraswati, dan Vishvakarma. Dalam sastra filsafat India, hamsa melambangkan jiwa individu atau roh universal. NHB menafsirkan burung hintha pada wadah persembahan Burma sebagai hamsa, tunggangan Brahma.",
      "en": "It is associated with a mythical ability to separate milk from mixed milk and water. In iconography it is the vehicle of Brahma, Gayatri, Saraswati, and Vishvakarma.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "hamsa-bird-c04",
      "source_id": "hamsa-bird-s1",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hamsa-bird-c05",
      "source_id": "hamsa-bird-s1",
      "quote": "In Indian philosophical literature, hamsa represents the individual soul or spirit ... the \"Universal Soul or Supreme Spirit\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "id": "hamsa-bird-c06",
      "source_id": "hamsa-bird-s2",
      "quote": "The hintha bird at the top represents the goose (hamsa), the mount of Brahma",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kanthaka adalah kuda putih kesayangan Pangeran Siddhartha dalam legenda Buddha. Siddhartha menggunakannya dalam peristiwa penting sebelum melepaskan kehidupan duniawi.",
      "en": "Kanthaka is Prince Siddhartha's favorite white horse in Buddhist legend. Siddhartha uses him in major events before renouncing worldly life.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kanthaka-c01",
        "kanthaka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda menuturkan Kanthaka mati karena patah hati setelah perpisahan dengan Siddhartha. Ia menarik kereta ketika Channa mendampingi Siddhartha melihat empat pemandangan yang mendorong pelepasan dunia. Ia membawa Siddhartha keluar dari istana menuju hutan dekat sungai Anoma.",
      "en": "The legend recounts Kanthaka dying of a broken heart after parting from Siddhartha. He pulls the chariot when Channa accompanies Siddhartha to the four sights prompting renunciation. He carries Siddhartha out of the palace toward a forest by the Anoma river.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kanthaka-c03",
        "kanthaka-c04",
        "kanthaka-c05"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kanthaka-c01",
      "source_id": "kanthaka-s1",
      "quote": "favourite white horse and royal servant of Prince Siddhartha, who later became Gautama Buddha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kanthaka adalah kuda putih kesayangan Pangeran Siddhartha dalam legenda Buddha.",
        "en": "Kanthaka is Prince Siddhartha's favorite white horse in Buddhist legend."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanthaka-c02",
      "source_id": "kanthaka-s1",
      "quote": "Siddhartha used Kanthaka in all major events described in Buddhist texts prior to his renunciation of the world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Siddhartha menggunakannya dalam peristiwa penting sebelum melepaskan kehidupan duniawi.",
        "en": "Siddhartha uses him in major events before renouncing worldly life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanthaka-c03",
      "source_id": "kanthaka-s1",
      "quote": "Following the departure of Siddhartha, Kanthaka died of a broken heart.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menuturkan Kanthaka mati karena patah hati setelah perpisahan dengan Siddhartha.",
        "en": "The legend recounts Kanthaka dying of a broken heart after parting from Siddhartha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanthaka-c04",
      "source_id": "kanthaka-s1",
      "quote": "After Siddhartha's marriage, Kanthaka was the horse pulling the chariot when Channa, the head royal servant, accompanied Siddhartha around Kapilavastu. Siddhartha saw the Four sights here whilst meeting his subjects, which prompted his decision to renounce the world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menarik kereta ketika Channa mendampingi Siddhartha melihat empat pemandangan yang mendorong pelepasan dunia.",
        "en": "He pulls the chariot when Channa accompanies Siddhartha to the four sights prompting renunciation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanthaka-c05",
      "source_id": "kanthaka-s1",
      "quote": "Kanthaka was the horse used by Siddhartha to escape from the palace and become an ascetic while the palace guards were asleep. ... to a forest by the edge of the Anoma River.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa Siddhartha keluar dari istana menuju hutan dekat sungai Anoma.",
        "en": "He carries Siddhartha out of the palace toward a forest by the Anoma river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kanthaka-c06",
      "source_id": "kanthaka-s2",
      "quote": "Around the maidan, but white Kantaka\r\n     Left even the fleetest far behind",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam adaptasi puisi Arnold, Kantaka putih melampaui kuda lain dalam perlombaan calon pasangan Yasodhara.",
        "en": "In Arnold's verse adaptation white Kantaka outpaces other horses in the contest for Yasodhara."
      }
    }
  ],

      "source_id": "karkotaka-s1",
      "quote": "Karkotaka once deceived Narada, the divine sage, in a game of chance. Angered, Narada cursed him that he would remain stationary in the forest until he is saved by King Nala.       "source_id": "karkotaka-s1",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Nala menyelamatkannya dari kebakaran setelah ia mengecil sebesar ibu jari.",
        "en": "Nala saves him from a fire after he shrinks to thumb size."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karkotaka-c06",
      "source_id": "karkotaka-s2",
      "quote": "My\r\npoison, too, will cause unceasing anguish to the evil one\r\nwho possesseth thy soul; ... My\r\npoison will harm thee not,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1978",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan racun Karkotaka menyiksa Kali yang merasuki Nala, tetapi tidak membahayakan Nala.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts Karkotaka's poison tormenting Kali possessing Nala while leaving Nala unharmed."
      }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Manohara adalah kinnari, perempuan-burung, tokoh utama kisah Jataka. Kisahnya muncul dalam Pannasa Jataka, Divyavadana, dan relief Borobudur.",
      "en": "Manohara is a kinnari, a bird woman, and heroine of a Jataka tale. Her story appears in the Pannasa Jataka, Divyavadana, and Borobudur reliefs.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manohara-c01",
        "manohara-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia merupakan putri termuda dari tujuh putri raja Kinnara yang tinggal di Kailash. Seorang pemburu menangkapnya dan memberikannya kepada Sudhana, yang kemudian menikahinya. Ketika dituduh membawa kesialan dan diancam mati, ia terbang pulang dan meninggalkan cincin serta petunjuk bagi Sudhana. Sonobudoyo menjelaskan Manohara pada relief Borobudur digambarkan sebagai manusia utuh menyerupai bidadari.",
      "en": "She is the youngest of the Kinnara king's seven daughters living on Kailash. A hunter captures her and gives her to Sudhana, who later marries her. When accused of bringing misfortune and threatened with death she flies home, leaving a ring and directions for Sudhana. Sonobudoyo explains that Manohara on Borobudur reliefs is depicted as a complete human resembling an angel.",
      "claim_ids": [

      "source_id": "manohara-s1",
      "quote": "Manoharā (Pali: \"captivating\", \"heart-stealing\") or Manoharī is the kinnari (half-woman, half-bird) heroine of one of the Jataka tales. Typically referred to as Manohara and Prince Sudhana, the legend appears in the Paññāsa Jātaka, Divyavadana and is documented by stone reliefs at Borobodur.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya muncul dalam Pannasa Jataka, Divyavadana, dan relief Borobudur.",

      }

    {
      "id": "manohara-c03",
      "source_id": "manohara-s1",
      "quote": "Manohara, the youngest of seven daughters of the Kimnara king, lives on Mount Kailash. One day, she travels to the human realm. She is caught by a hunter (using a magic noose in some versions) who gives her to Prince Sudhana. Son of King Adityavamsa and Queen Chandradevi, Sudhana is a renowned archer and heir to the Panchala kingdom.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan putri termuda dari tujuh putri raja Kinnara yang tinggal di Kailash.",
        "en": "She is the youngest of the Kinnara king's seven daughters living on Kailash."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manohara-c04",
      "source_id": "manohara-s1",
      "quote": "She is caught by a hunter (using a magic noose in some versions) who gives her to Prince Sudhana. ... The prince falls in love with Manohara, and they get married.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seorang pemburu menangkapnya dan memberikannya kepada Sudhana, yang kemudian menikahinya.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manohara-c05",
      "source_id": "manohara-s1",
      "quote": "Later, when the prince is away in battle, Manohara is accused by the royal counselor of bringing bad luck to the city and is threatened with death. She flies away, back to the Kimnara kingdom. She leaves behind a ring and the directions to reach the Kimnara kingdom so that Prince Sudhana can follow her.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika dituduh membawa kesialan dan diancam mati, ia terbang pulang dan meninggalkan cincin serta petunjuk bagi Sudhana.",
        "en": "When accused of bringing misfortune and threatened with death she flies home, leaving a ring and directions for Sudhana."

      "source_id": "manohara-s2",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "id": "Aghasura adalah asura dalam Bhagavata Purana dan salah satu jenderal Kansa.",
        "en": "Aghasura is an asura in the Bhagavata Purana and one of Kansa's generals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aghasura-c02",
      "source_id": "aghasura-s1",
      "quote": "the elder brother of the demoness Putana and Bakasura.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel menyebutnya kakak Putana dan Bakasura.",
        "en": "The article calls him Putana and Bakasura's elder brother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aghasura-c03",
      "source_id": "aghasura-s1",
      "quote": "King Kamsa made many attempts on the life of Krishna, all of them failing. He then sent Aghasura to kill Krishna, a deed which Aghasura willingly attempted to carry out, knowing that his younger siblings Putana and Bakasura were killed by Krishna. He assumed the form of the 8-mile-long serpent, disguising his open mouth against a mountain.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berubah menjadi ular sepanjang delapan mil untuk membunuh Krishna.",
        "en": "He becomes an eight mile long serpent to kill Krishna."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aghasura-c04",
      "source_id": "aghasura-s1",
      "quote": "All the cowherd boys entered the mouth of the demon, mistaking it to be a cavern.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anak-anak penggembala memasuki mulutnya karena mengira mulut itu gua.",
        "en": "Cowherd boys enter his mouth, mistaking it for a cavern."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aghasura-c05",
      "source_id": "aghasura-s1",
      "quote": "Krishna entered the serpent upon his arrival and then increased the size of his own body. In response, the demon also extended his own body's size, but started suffocating as Krishna was expanding more quickly than him,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Krishna membesarkan tubuh di dalam ular sehingga Aghasura mati tercekik.",
        "en": "Krishna enlarges his body inside the serpent until Aghasura suffocates."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "aghasura-c06",
      "source_id": "aghasura-s2",
      "quote": "Aghāsura, who had been sent by Kaṁsa, was the younger brother of Pūtanā and Bakāsura.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Vedabase menyebut Aghasura adik Putana dan Bakasura, berbeda dari ringkasan artikel.",
        "en": "The Vedabase translation calls Aghasura Putana and Bakasura's younger brother, differing from the article summary."
      }
    }
  ],

      "en": "Sarama or Devashuni is the gods' female dog in Hindu mythology. In the Rigveda she helps Indra recover divine cows stolen by the Panis.",

      ]

      "id": "Teks Rigveda awal tidak langsung menyebutnya anjing; tafsir Weda belakangan menyebutnya demikian. Ia disebut ibu dua anjing Yama bermata empat dan berbulu belang. Sebutan Devashuni berarti anjing betina ilahi atau anjing betina para dewa. Dalam Mahabharata terjemahan Ganguli, Janamejaya mencari pendeta yang dapat menetralkan kutukan Sarama.",

      "id": "sarama-c01",
      "source_id": "sarama-s1",
      "quote": "is a female dog of the gods in Hindu mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sarama atau Devashuni adalah anjing betina para dewa dalam mitologi Hindu.",
        "en": "Sarama or Devashuni is the gods' female dog in Hindu mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarama-c02",
      "source_id": "sarama-s1",
      "quote": "Sarama (Sanskrit: सरमा, Saramā) or Devashuni (Sanskrit: देव-शुनी, Devaśunī) is a female dog of the gods in Hindu mythology. She first appears in one of Hinduism's earliest texts, the Rig Veda, in which she helps the king of the gods Indra to recover divine cows stolen by the Panis asuras. This legend is alluded to in many later texts, and Sarama is often associated with Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Rigveda ia membantu Indra menemukan sapi ilahi yang dicuri para Pani.",
        "en": "In the Rigveda she helps Indra recover divine cows stolen by the Panis."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarama-c03",
      "source_id": "sarama-s1",
      "quote": "Early Rig Vedic works do not directly call Sarama a \"dog,\" but her actions, behavior, and onomatopoeia in R.V. 10.108 provide a vivid description of a dog's behavior, and dogs in the Rig Veda are often given the name Sarameya—daughters of Sarama. In addition later Vedic mythologies and interpretations also directly call Sarama as a dog.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Rigveda awal tidak langsung menyebutnya anjing; tafsir Weda belakangan menyebutnya demikian.",
        "en": "Early Rigvedic texts do not directly call her a dog; later Vedic interpretations do."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarama-c04",
      "source_id": "sarama-s1",
      "quote": "the mother of all dogs, in particular of the two four-eyed brindle dogs of the god Yama,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut ibu dua anjing Yama bermata empat dan berbulu belang.",
        "en": "She is called mother of Yama's two four eyed brindled dogs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarama-c05",
      "source_id": "sarama-s1",
      "quote": "There are two epithets for Sarama in the original Rig Veda. Firstly, she is described as supadi, which means \"having good feet\", \"fair-footed\" or \"quick\", an epithet only used for Sarama in the text. Her other epithet is subhaga – \"the fortunate one\", or \"the beloved one\" – a common epithet of the Ushas, the Dawn.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan Devashuni berarti anjing betina ilahi atau anjing betina para dewa.",
        "en": "The epithet Devashuni means divine bitch or bitch of the gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sarama-c06",
      "source_id": "sarama-s2",
      "quote": "Janamejaya, thus addressed by the celestial bitch, Sarama, became\r\n      exceedingly alarmed and dejected. ... searching for a\r\n      Purohita who could by procuring absolution for his sin, neutralise the\r\n      effect of the curse.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 94",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Mahabharata terjemahan Ganguli, Janamejaya mencari pendeta yang dapat menetralkan kutukan Sarama.",
        "en": "In Ganguli's Mahabharata translation Janamejaya seeks a priest to neutralize Sarama's curse."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shakchunni adalah hantu perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Bengali, roh perempuan Hindu yang mati sebelum suaminya.",
    "en": "Shakchunni is a female ghost in Bengali folklore, the spirit of a Hindu woman dying before her husband.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "shakchunni-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shakchunni adalah hantu perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Bengali, roh perempuan Hindu yang mati sebelum suaminya. Ia mempertahankan gelang kerang shankha dan vermilion sindoor, lambang perkawinan.",
      "en": "Shakchunni is a female ghost in Bengali folklore, the spirit of a Hindu woman dying before her husband. She retains shankha conch bangles and sindoor vermilion, symbols of marriage.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shakchunni-c01",
        "shakchunni-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dikisahkan merasuki perempuan menikah untuk mengalami lagi kehidupan rumah tangga. Dalam banyak kisah, ojha akhirnya mengusir atau mengalahkan roh ini. Deskripsi artikel menyebut kulitnya kehijauan dan kain sari merah-putih. Catatan Lal Behari Day menyebut Sankchinni atau Sankhachurni berkulit putih dan tampak seperti kain putih di dekat pohon pada tengah malam. Dalam kisah A Ghostly Wife, Sankchinni menawan seorang istri di lubang pohon dan mengenakan pakaiannya untuk memasuki rumah.",
      "en": "She is said to possess married women to relive household life. In many tales an ojha eventually expels or defeats the spirit. The article's description gives her greenish skin and a red and white sari. Lal Behari Day's note calls Sankchinnis or Sankhachurnis white complexioned, appearing like white cloth near trees at midnight. In A Ghostly Wife a Sankchinni imprisons a wife in a tree hollow and wears her clothes to enter the house.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shakchunni-c03",
        "shakchunni-c04",
        "shakchunni-c05",
        "shakchunni-c06",
        "shakchunni-c07"

    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c01",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s1",
      "quote": "Shakchunni ... is a female ghost in Bengali folklore, ... The term refers to the spirit of a married Hindu woman who died while still married (i.e., before her husband).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shakchunni adalah hantu perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Bengali, roh perempuan Hindu yang mati sebelum suaminya.",
        "en": "Shakchunni is a female ghost in Bengali folklore, the spirit of a Hindu woman dying before her husband."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c02",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s1",
      "quote": "The word Shakchunni is derived from the Sanskrit term Shankhachurni. In Bengali Hindu tradition, married women traditionally wear conch-shell bangles (shankha) and vermilion (sindoor) as symbols of marriage. The Shakchunni is believed to retain these symbols even after death, reflecting her continued attachment to married life.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mempertahankan gelang kerang shankha dan vermilion sindoor, lambang perkawinan.",
        "en": "She retains shankha conch bangles and sindoor vermilion, symbols of marriage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c03",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s1",
      "quote": "According to folklore, a Shakchunni is the spirit of a married woman whose desires and attachments to domestic life remain unfulfilled at the time of her death. She is often portrayed as longing for marital happiness and household life, and is said to possess or influence married women, especially those from affluent households, in order to relive such experiences.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikisahkan merasuki perempuan menikah untuk mengalami lagi kehidupan rumah tangga.",
        "en": "She is said to possess married women to relive household life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c04",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s1",
      "quote": "Stories featuring Shakchunni appear in traditional Bengali folktales, including those found in collections such as Thakurmar Jhuli. In many narratives, an exorcist (ojha) ultimately defeats or expels her spirit.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam banyak kisah, ojha akhirnya mengusir atau mengalahkan roh ini.",
        "en": "In many tales an ojha eventually expels or defeats the spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c05",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s1",
      "quote": "Shakchunnis are typically described as having greenish skin, ... They wear traditional red-and-white saris",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Deskripsi artikel menyebut kulitnya kehijauan dan kain sari merah-putih.",
        "en": "The article's description gives her greenish skin and a red and white sari."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c06",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s2",
      "quote": "1\nSankchinnis or Sankhachurnis are female ghosts of white\r\ncomplexion. They usually stand at the dead of night at the foot of\r\ntrees, and look like sheets of white cloth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 316",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan Lal Behari Day menyebut Sankchinni atau Sankhachurni berkulit putih dan tampak seperti kain putih di dekat pohon pada tengah malam.",
        "en": "Lal Behari Day's note calls Sankchinnis or Sankhachurnis white complexioned, appearing like white cloth near trees at midnight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shakchunni-c07",
      "source_id": "shakchunni-s2",
      "quote": "thrust her into a hole in the trunk. ... The ghost put on the clothes of\r\nthe woman and went into the house of the Brahman.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 312",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "In A Ghostly Wife a Sankchinni imprisons a wife in a tree hollow and wears her clothes to enter the house."

      "id": "Lavana atau Lavanasura adalah asura yang dibunuh Shatrughna dalam Ramayana. Ia merupakan putra Madhu dan Kumbhini.",
      "en": "Lavana or Lavanasura is an asura killed by Shatrughna in the Ramayana. He is Madhu and Kumbhini's son.",

    {
      "id": "Ia mengganggu para resi dan menghancurkan ritual sehingga mereka meminta perlindungan Rama. Madhu mewariskan kepadanya trisula anugerah Shiva. Rama memperingatkan Shatrughna untuk melawannya saat ia tidak memegang trisula yang tak terkalahkan. Dalam pertarungan, Lavana mencabut pohon dan melemparkannya ke Shatrughna.",
      "en": "He troubles sages and destroys rituals so they ask Rama for protection. Madhu passes down to him the trident granted by Shiva. Rama warns Shatrughna to fight him while he is without the invincible trident. During the fight Lavana uproots trees and throws them at Shatrughna.",

      "quote": "Lavana (Sanskrit: लवणासुर, romanized: Lavaṇāsura) is an asura in Hinduism. He is slain by Shatrughna, the youngest brother of Rama, in the Hindu epic Ramayana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lavana atau Lavanasura adalah asura yang dibunuh Shatrughna dalam Ramayana.",
        "en": "Lavana or Lavanasura is an asura killed by Shatrughna in the Ramayana."

    {
      "id": "lawdasura-c02",
      "source_id": "lawdasura-s1",
      "quote": "Lavana was the son of an asura king named Madhu and his wife, Kumbhini.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan putra Madhu dan Kumbhini.",
        "en": "He is Madhu and Kumbhini's son."
      }

    {
      "id": "lawdasura-c03",
      "source_id": "lawdasura-s1",
      "quote": "During Rama's reign, while peace prevailed in most places, Lavanasura continued to torment the innocent and destroy many sacrifices of sages and terrify them in many ways. Many kings were defeated by him and they were all afraid. Therefore, one day, the sages headed by sage Chyavana (a descendant of sage Bhrigu), came all the way from Madhupuri to Rama with a plea to protect them.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengganggu para resi dan menghancurkan ritual sehingga mereka meminta perlindungan Rama.",
        "en": "He troubles sages and destroys rituals so they ask Rama for protection."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lawdasura-c04",
      "source_id": "lawdasura-s1",
      "quote": "Madhu handed everything over to his son including his trident",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Madhu passes down to him the trident granted by Shiva."

    },
    {
      "id": "lawdasura-c05",
      "source_id": "lawdasura-s1",
      "quote": "Rama warned his brother to find a way to fight with Lavana without his holding the invincible trident.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rama memperingatkan Shatrughna untuk melawannya saat ia tidak memegang trisula yang tak terkalahkan.",
        "en": "Rama warns Shatrughna to fight him while he is without the invincible trident."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lawdasura-c06",
      "source_id": "lawdasura-s1",
      "quote": "Lavana uprooted many trees and threw them on Shatrughna, and a great battle ensued.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam pertarungan, Lavana mencabut pohon dan melemparkannya ke Shatrughna.",

      "id": "Pakhangba adalah dewa primordial berwujud naga dalam mitologi Meitei dan Sanamahism Manipur. Figurnya muncul dalam lambang kerajaan Manipur, berasal dari ilustrasi mitos paphal.",
      "en": "Pakhangba is a primordial dragon deity in Meitei mythology and Manipur's Sanamahism. His figure appears in Manipur royal heraldry derived from mythical paphal illustrations.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pakhangba-c01",
        "pakhangba-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Identitasnya sering dipadukan dengan raja pertama Ningthouja yang juga bernama Pakhangba. Atas petunjuk ibunya, ia mengelilingi takhta ayahnya sebagai padanan mengelilingi alam semesta dan memperoleh takhta. Ia digambarkan sebagai ular dengan tanduk rusa sangai. N. Khelchandra Singh menuturkan versi raja-dewa Pakhangba yang bergerak sebagai naga pada malam hari dan dikenal sebagai Sanahing Pakhangba.",
      "en": "His identity is often fused with the first Ningthouja ruler also named Pakhangba. Following his mother's advice he circles his father's throne as equivalent to circling the universe and gains the throne. He is depicted as a serpent with sangai deer antlers. N. Khelchandra Singh recounts a god king Pakhangba version moving as a dragon at night and called Sanahing Pakhangba.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "pakhangba-c01",
      "source_id": "pakhangba-s1",
      "quote": "is a primordial deity, often represented in the form of a dragon, in Meitei mythology and Sanamahism, the indigenous religion of Manipur.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pakhangba adalah dewa primordial berwujud naga dalam mitologi Meitei dan Sanamahism Manipur.",
        "en": "Pakhangba is a primordial dragon deity in Meitei mythology and Manipur's Sanamahism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pakhangba-c02",
      "source_id": "pakhangba-s1",
      "quote": "Pakhangba (Meitei: ꯄꯥꯈꯪꯕ, lit. 'the one who knows his father') is a primordial deity, often represented in the form of a dragon, in Meitei mythology and Sanamahism, the indigenous religion of Manipur. He is depicted in the heraldry of the Manipur kingdom, which originated in paphal (Meitei: ꯄꯥꯐꯜ), mythical illustrations of the deity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Figurnya muncul dalam lambang kerajaan Manipur, berasal dari ilustrasi mitos paphal.",
        "en": "His figure appears in Manipur royal heraldry derived from mythical paphal illustrations."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pakhangba-c03",
      "source_id": "pakhangba-s1",
      "quote": "The identity of the deity is often fused with Nongda Lairen Pakhangba, the first ruler of the Ningthouja dynasty. The title Pakhangba was also used by other kings in the history of Manipur.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Identitasnya sering dipadukan dengan raja pertama Ningthouja yang juga bernama Pakhangba.",
        "en": "His identity is often fused with the first Ningthouja ruler also named Pakhangba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pakhangba-c04",
      "source_id": "pakhangba-s1",
      "quote": "His mother Leimarel Sidabi, said that circling his father's throne is equivalent to the circling of the entire universe. So, he circled the throne, and his father was satisfied with Pakhangba's knowledge.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Atas petunjuk ibunya, ia mengelilingi takhta ayahnya sebagai padanan mengelilingi alam semesta dan memperoleh takhta.",
        "en": "Following his mother's advice he circles his father's throne as equivalent to circling the universe and gains the throne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pakhangba-c05",
      "source_id": "pakhangba-s1",
      "quote": "Pakhangba is depicted as a serpent with the antlers of a sangai, or brow-antlered deer,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai ular dengan tanduk rusa sangai.",
        "en": "He is depicted as a serpent with sangai deer antlers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pakhangba-c06",
      "source_id": "pakhangba-s2",
      "quote": "Because of his divine power to be able to move about at night as dragon he is also known as Sanahing Pakhangba.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "N. Khelchandra Singh menuturkan versi raja-dewa Pakhangba yang bergerak sebagai naga pada malam hari dan dikenal sebagai Sanahing Pakhangba.",
        "en": "N. Khelchandra Singh recounts a god king Pakhangba version moving as a dragon at night and called Sanahing Pakhangba."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "World Turtle adalah motif kura-kura raksasa yang menopang atau memuat dunia.",
    "en": "The World Turtle is a motif of a giant turtle supporting or containing the world.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "world-turtle-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "World Turtle adalah motif kura-kura raksasa yang menopang atau memuat dunia. Motifnya ditemukan dalam tradisi Hindu, Tiongkok, dan sebagian masyarakat pribumi Amerika.",
      "en": "The World Turtle is a motif of a giant turtle supporting or containing the world. The motif occurs in Hindu, Chinese, and some Indigenous American traditions.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "world-turtle-c01",
        "world-turtle-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi Hindu, kura-kura dunia dikenal sebagai Akupara atau Chukwa. Versi Tiongkok menuturkan Nuwa memakai kaki kura-kura Ao untuk menopang langit setelah Gong Gong merusak Gunung Buzhou. Kisah Great Turtle Lenape dicatat Jasper Danckaerts pada 1678–1680. Dalam penuturan Mackenzie, gerak anggota badan Kurma membuat Shesha menguap dan menimbulkan gempa.",
      "en": "In Hindu tradition the world turtle is known as Akupara or Chukwa. The Chinese version tells of Nuwa using Ao's legs to support the sky after Gong Gong damages Mount Buzhou. The Lenape Great Turtle story was recorded by Jasper Danckaerts in 1678–1680. In Mackenzie's retelling movement of Kurma's limbs makes Shesha yawn and causes earthquakes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "world-turtle-c03",
        "world-turtle-c04",
        "world-turtle-c05",
        "world-turtle-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Motif lintas budaya ini dicatat menurut versi masing-masing; bukan satu makhluk dengan silsilah tunggal.",
    "en": "This cross cultural motif is presented by separate versions rather than a creature with one genealogy.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "world-turtle-c02",
      "world-turtle-c04",
      "world-turtle-c05",
      "world-turtle-c06"
    ]
  },

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-s1",

      "published": null,

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-c01",
      "source_id": "world-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "is a mytheme of a giant turtle (or tortoise) supporting or containing the world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "World Turtle adalah motif kura-kura raksasa yang menopang atau memuat dunia.",
        "en": "The World Turtle is a motif of a giant turtle supporting or containing the world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-c02",
      "source_id": "world-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "It occurs in Hinduism, Chinese mythology, and the mythologies of some of the indigenous peoples of the Americas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Motifnya ditemukan dalam tradisi Hindu, Tiongkok, dan sebagian masyarakat pribumi Amerika.",
        "en": "The motif occurs in Hindu, Chinese, and some Indigenous American traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-c03",
      "source_id": "world-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "The World Turtle in Hinduism is known as Akūpāra (Sanskrit: अकूपार), or sometimes Chukwa.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Hindu, kura-kura dunia dikenal sebagai Akupara atau Chukwa.",
        "en": "In Hindu tradition the world turtle is known as Akupara or Chukwa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-c04",
      "source_id": "world-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "In the Chinese mythology, the creator goddess Nüwa cut the legs off the giant sea turtle Ao (simplified Chinese: 鳌; traditional Chinese: 鰲; pinyin: áo) and used them to prop up the sky after Gong Gong damaged Mount Buzhou, which had previously supported the heavens.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Tiongkok menuturkan Nuwa memakai kaki kura-kura Ao untuk menopang langit setelah Gong Gong merusak Gunung Buzhou.",
        "en": "The Chinese version tells of Nuwa using Ao's legs to support the sky after Gong Gong damages Mount Buzhou."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-c05",
      "source_id": "world-turtle-s1",
      "quote": "The Lenape creation story of the \"Great Turtle\" was first recorded between 1678 and 1680 by Jasper Danckaerts. The belief is shared by other indigenous peoples of the Northeastern Woodlands, most notably those of the Haudenosaunee confederacy, and the Anishinaabeg.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Great Turtle Lenape dicatat Jasper Danckaerts pada 1678–1680.",
        "en": "The Lenape Great Turtle story was recorded by Jasper Danckaerts in 1678–1680."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "world-turtle-c06",
      "source_id": "world-turtle-s2",
      "quote": "When the tortoise moves its limbs, Shesha is\r\nroused to yawn; thus are earthquakes caused.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 726",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam penuturan Mackenzie, gerak anggota badan Kurma membuat Shesha menguap dan menimbulkan gempa.",
        "en": "In Mackenzie's retelling movement of Kurma's limbs makes Shesha yawn and causes earthquakes."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Akuma adalah roh jahat dalam cerita rakyat Jepang yang juga disebut ma. Pemakaian awal kata akuma terutama ditemukan dalam teks Buddha.",
      "en": "Akuma is an evil spirit in Japanese folklore also called ma. Early uses of the word akuma are mainly found in Buddhist texts.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "akuma-folklore-c01",
        "akuma-folklore-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kata itu juga muncul dalam karya sastra zaman Heian. Akuma digambarkan berkepala berapi, bermata menyala, dan membawa pedang. Melihatnya dipercaya menjadi pertanda kesialan. Ia dikatakan merasuki orang dan menyebabkan bencana alam.",

    {
      "id": "Strabo menggambarkannya sebagai keturunan campuran serigala dan anjing. Pliny menggambarkannya sebagai campuran hiena dan singa. Deskripsi Pliny menyebut rahangnya memiliki satu punggung tulang sebagai gigi tanpa gusi. Terjemahan Natural History Pliny menyebut corocotta meniru suara manusia dan ternak.",

      "en": "Mahoraga are a group of reptilian deities in Hindu, Buddhist, and Jain traditions. Depictions can have reptilian bodies from the waist down.",

    {
      "id": "Mereka termasuk delapan golongan dewa pelindung Dharma. Mereka dikisahkan sebagai ular besar di bawah tanah yang memutar bumi dan kadang menyebabkan gempa. Mereka juga dikaitkan dengan musik instrumental. Dalam terjemahan Lotus Sutra Soothill, mahoraga hadir bersama makhluk lain dan menghormati Buddha.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mahoraga-c01",
      "source_id": "mahoraga-s1",
      "quote": "are a race of deities in Hinduism, Buddhism, and Jainism. ... a race of primordial reptilian beings",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mahoraga adalah kelompok dewa reptil dalam tradisi Hindu, Buddha, dan Jain.",
        "en": "Mahoraga are a group of reptilian deities in Hindu, Buddhist, and Jain traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahoraga-c02",
      "source_id": "mahoraga-s1",
      "quote": "Like the nāga, they are often depicted as anthropomorphic beings with reptilian bodies from the waist down.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya dapat memiliki tubuh reptil dari pinggang ke bawah.",
        "en": "Depictions can have reptilian bodies from the waist down."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahoraga-c03",
      "source_id": "mahoraga-s1",
      "quote": "The Mahoraga are one of the eight classes of deities (aṣṭasenā) that are said to protect the Dharma. They are described as huge subterranean serpents who lie on their sides and rotate the earth, which occasionally causes earthquakes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka termasuk delapan golongan dewa pelindung Dharma.",
        "en": "They belong to eight classes of deities protecting the Dharma."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahoraga-c04",
      "source_id": "mahoraga-s1",
      "quote": "The Mahoraga are one of the eight classes of deities (aṣṭasenā) that are said to protect the Dharma. They are described as huge subterranean serpents who lie on their sides and rotate the earth, which occasionally causes earthquakes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dikisahkan sebagai ular besar di bawah tanah yang memutar bumi dan kadang menyebabkan gempa.",
        "en": "They are described as huge underground serpents rotating the earth and sometimes causing earthquakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahoraga-c05",
      "source_id": "mahoraga-s1",
      "quote": "Like the kinnara, the Mahoraga are also associated with instrumental music. They are understood as being associated with large serpents such as pythons, while the nāgas are more closely related to the cobra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka juga dikaitkan dengan musik instrumental.",
        "en": "They are also associated with instrumental music."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mahoraga-c06",
      "source_id": "mahoraga-s2",
      "quote": "Then the monks and nuns, male and female disciples, gods, nagas,\r\nyakshas, gandharvas, asuras, garudas, kinnaras, mahoragas, and others,\r\nall the great assembly of beings, human and not human, rejoice greatly,\r\neach doffing his robe and offering it in homage to the Buddha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 151",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam terjemahan Lotus Sutra Soothill, mahoraga hadir bersama makhluk lain dan menghormati Buddha.",
        "en": "In Soothill's Lotus Sutra translation mahoragas join other beings in honoring the Buddha."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Manibhadra adalah salah satu yaksha utama dan dewa populer di India kuno.",
    "en": "Manibhadra is a major yaksha and a popular deity in ancient India.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Manibhadra adalah salah satu yaksha utama dan dewa populer di India kuno. Ia sering digambarkan memegang kantong uang.",
      "en": "Manibhadra is a major yaksha and a popular deity in ancient India. He is often depicted holding a bag of money.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manibhadra-c01",
        "manibhadra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah Buddha, ia dikatakan menghuni Manimalachaitya di Magadha. Sastra Jain menyebutnya pemimpin yaksha utara, sementara Purnabhadra memimpin selatan. Di kalangan Jain Gujarat ia dipuja dengan tunggangan Airavata, gajah bergading banyak. Mackenzie mencatat Manibhadra dipuja oleh para pelancong dan menyerupai dewa kekayaan Kuvera.",
      "en": "In Buddhist accounts he is said to dwell at Manimalachaitya in Magadha. Jain literature calls him leader of northern yakshas while Purnabhadra leads the south. Among Gujarat Jains he is worshipped with Airavata, a multi tusked elephant mount. Mackenzie records Manibhadra being worshipped by travelers and resembling Kuvera, the god of wealth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manibhadra-c03",
        "manibhadra-c04",
        "manibhadra-c05",
        "manibhadra-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "manibhadra-c01",
      "source_id": "manibhadra-s1",
      "quote": "Maṇibhadra (Sanskrit: 'as excellent as a jewel') is one of the major yakshas. He was a popular deity in ancient India.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Manibhadra adalah salah satu yaksha utama dan dewa populer di India kuno.",
        "en": "Manibhadra is a major yaksha and a popular deity in ancient India."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manibhadra-c02",
      "source_id": "manibhadra-s1",
      "quote": "Manibhadra was often shown with a bag of money in his hand.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering digambarkan memegang kantong uang.",
        "en": "He is often depicted holding a bag of money."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manibhadra-c03",
      "source_id": "manibhadra-s1",
      "quote": "In Samyukta Nikaya,  Manibhadra is said to reside in the Manimala chaitya  in Magadha. Yaksha Manibhadra is invoked in The Exalted Manibhadra’s Dhārani.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah Buddha, ia dikatakan menghuni Manimalachaitya di Magadha.",
        "en": "In Buddhist accounts he is said to dwell at Manimalachaitya in Magadha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manibhadra-c04",
      "source_id": "manibhadra-s1",
      "quote": "In Sūryaprajñapti, a Manibhadra chairya in Mithila is mentioned. Yakshas are referred to in the Harivamsa Purana (783 A.D.) of Jinasena made the beginning of this concept. Among them, Manibhadra and Purnabadra yakshas and Bahuputrika yakshini have been the most popular.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sastra Jain menyebutnya pemimpin yaksha utara, sementara Purnabhadra memimpin selatan.",
        "en": "Jain literature calls him leader of northern yakshas while Purnabhadra leads the south."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manibhadra-c05",
      "source_id": "manibhadra-s1",
      "quote": "Manibhadra Yaksha (or Vira) is a popular demigod among the Jains in Gujarat. ... he is shown with a multi-tusked elephant Airavata.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 10",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di kalangan Jain Gujarat ia dipuja dengan tunggangan Airavata, gajah bergading banyak.",
        "en": "Among Gujarat Jains he is worshipped with Airavata, a multi tusked elephant mount."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manibhadra-c06",
      "source_id": "manibhadra-s2",
      "quote": "Manibhadra, the demi-god, was worshipped by travellers, and resembles Kuvera,\r\ngod of wealth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1970",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mencatat Manibhadra dipuja oleh para pelancong dan menyerupai dewa kekayaan Kuvera.",
        "en": "Mackenzie records Manibhadra being worshipped by travelers and resembling Kuvera, the god of wealth."
      }
    }

        "en": "Sayana identifies him with Vayu, the wind, in interpreting RV 1.93.6."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matarisvan-c05",
      "source_id": "matarisvan-s1",
      "quote": "In the Atharvaveda and later, the word also has the meaning of \"air, wind, breeze\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Atharvaveda dan kemudian, kata itu juga bermakna udara, angin, atau embusan.",
        "en": "In the Atharvaveda and later the word also means air, wind, or breeze."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "matarisvan-c06",
      "source_id": "matarisvan-s2",
      "quote": "brought Agni to Bhrigu\r\nas a gift, precious like wealth",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 213",
      "context": "historical-record",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Navagunjara adalah makhluk legenda Hindu gabungan sembilan hewan.",
    "en": "Navagunjara is a Hindu legendary creature composed of nine animals.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "navagunjara-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Navagunjara adalah makhluk legenda Hindu gabungan sembilan hewan. Ia menjadi motif umum seni Pattachitra Odisha.",
      "en": "Navagunjara is a Hindu legendary creature composed of nine animals. He is a common motif in Odisha's Pattachitra art.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "navagunjara-c01",
        "navagunjara-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dianggap wujud Vishnu atau Krishna dan varian Vishvarupa. Kisahnya dituturkan dalam Mahabharata Odia karya Sarala Dasa. Ia berkepala ayam jantan, dengan kaki gajah, harimau, dan rusa atau kuda serta lengan manusia membawa teratai atau roda. Arjuna mula-mula hendak menembaknya, kemudian mengenali manifestasi Vishnu dan menunduk. Bernard Cesarone menjelaskan tema nava-gunjara sebagai Vishnu atau Krishna dalam gabungan sembilan hewan.",
      "en": "He is considered a form of Vishnu or Krishna and a variant of Vishvarupa. His story is told in Sarala Dasa's Odia Mahabharata. He has a rooster's head, elephant, tiger, and deer or horse legs, and a human arm carrying a lotus or wheel. Arjuna first aims to shoot him, then recognizes Vishnu's manifestation and bows. Bernard Cesarone explains the nava-gunjara theme as Vishnu or Krishna in a composite of nine animals.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "navagunjara-c03",
        "navagunjara-c04",
        "navagunjara-c05",
        "navagunjara-c06",
        "navagunjara-c07"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c01",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s1",
      "quote": "Navaguñjara (Sanskrit: नवगुञ्जर), or Nabaguñjara (Odia: ନବଗୁଞ୍ଜର) is a magical legendary creature composed of nine different animals in Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Navagunjara adalah makhluk legenda Hindu gabungan sembilan hewan.",
        "en": "Navagunjara is a Hindu legendary creature composed of nine animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c02",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s1",
      "quote": "The animal is a common motif in the Pattachitra style of painting, of the Eastern Indian state of Odisha. It is considered an astral form of the god Vishnu, or of Krishna, who is considered an avatar of Lord Vishnu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi motif umum seni Pattachitra Odisha.",
        "en": "He is a common motif in Odisha's Pattachitra art."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c03",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s1",
      "quote": "It is considered an astral form of the god Vishnu, or of Krishna, ... It is considered a variant of the Vishvarupa",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap wujud Vishnu atau Krishna dan varian Vishvarupa.",
        "en": "He is considered a form of Vishnu or Krishna and a variant of Vishvarupa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c04",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s1",
      "quote": "The version of the Hindu epic Mahabharata, written by the Odia poet Sarala Dasa, narrates the legend of Navagunjara;",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya dituturkan dalam Mahabharata Odia karya Sarala Dasa.",
        "en": "His story is told in Sarala Dasa's Odia Mahabharata."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c05",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s1",
      "quote": "Navagunjara has the head of a rooster, and stands on three feet, those of an elephant, tiger and deer or horse; the fourth limb is a raised human arm carrying a lotus or a wheel.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berkepala ayam jantan, dengan kaki gajah, harimau, dan rusa atau kuda serta lengan manusia membawa teratai atau roda.",
        "en": "He has a rooster's head, elephant, tiger, and deer or horse legs, and a human arm carrying a lotus or wheel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c06",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s1",
      "quote": "Initially, Arjuna was terrified as well as mesmerized by the strange creature and raises his bow to shoot it. Finally, Arjuna realizes that Navagunjara is a manifestation of Vishnu and drops his weapons, bowing before Navagunjara.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Arjuna mula-mula hendak menembaknya, kemudian mengenali manifestasi Vishnu dan menunduk.",
        "en": "Arjuna first aims to shoot him, then recognizes Vishnu's manifestation and bows."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "navagunjara-c07",
      "source_id": "navagunjara-s2",
      "quote": "Vishnu or Krishna is shown as a composite creature of \r\n        nine different animals",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bernard Cesarone menjelaskan tema nava-gunjara sebagai Vishnu atau Krishna dalam gabungan sembilan hewan.",
        "en": "Bernard Cesarone explains the nava-gunjara theme as Vishnu or Krishna in a composite of nine animals."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Acheri adalah roh gadis dalam cerita rakyat wilayah Kumaon di Himalaya selatan.",
    "en": "An Acheri is a girl spirit in Kumaon folklore in the southern Himalayas.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "acheri-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Acheri adalah roh gadis dalam cerita rakyat wilayah Kumaon di Himalaya selatan. Sejumlah kisah menghubungkannya dengan gadis atau perempuan yang mati secara keras.",
      "en": "An Acheri is a girl spirit in Kumaon folklore in the southern Himalayas. Some accounts associate her with girls or women dying violently.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "acheri-c01",
        "acheri-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia tinggal di gunung pada siang hari dan turun bersuka ria saat senja. Bayangannya dipercaya menularkan penyakit kepada anak-anak. Catatan Crooke dan Atkinson mengaitkan benang merah di leher dengan perlindungan dari selesma dan gondok. Crooke justru menyebut pakaian merah memancing kemarahannya, berbeda dari benang pelindung.",
      "en": "She dwells in the mountains by day and descends to revel at dusk. Her shadow is believed to inflict disease on children. Crooke and Atkinson's accounts associate a scarlet neck thread with protection from colds and goitre. Crooke instead calls red garments a target of her anger, distinct from the protective thread.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "acheri-c03",
        "acheri-c04",
        "acheri-c05",
        "acheri-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [
    {
      "ability_id": null,
      "name": {
        "id": "Bayangan pembawa penyakit dalam kepercayaan",
        "en": "Disease bearing shadow in belief"
      },
      "description": {
        "id": "Bayangan pembawa penyakit dalam kepercayaan",
        "en": "Disease bearing shadow in belief"
      },
      "claim_ids": [
        "acheri-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "weaknesses": [],
  "variants": [],
  "stories": [],

  "sources": [

      "id": "acheri-s1",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "acheri-c01",
      "source_id": "acheri-s1",
      "quote": "An Acheri is a spirit of a young girl in the folklore of the southern Himalayas, specifically regions around Kumaon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Acheri adalah roh gadis dalam cerita rakyat wilayah Kumaon di Himalaya selatan.",
        "en": "An Acheri is a girl spirit in Kumaon folklore in the southern Himalayas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "acheri-c02",
      "source_id": "acheri-s1",
      "quote": "An Acheri is a spirit of a young girl (and in many accounts women) who died a violent death. Acheris are also referred to as \"hill fairies\", \"Bhúts\", and \"hill godlings\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah kisah menghubungkannya dengan gadis atau perempuan yang mati secara keras.",
        "en": "Some accounts associate her with girls or women dying violently."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "acheri-c03",
      "source_id": "acheri-s1",
      "quote": "It is said that they dwell during the day in the mountains or hilltops, and at dusk they descend to hold revel. It is often unwise to visit the places where the revels, regardless of if the Acheri are present or not. They are also said to bring disease to children (and sometimes others) who come in contact with their shadow (Cháyá).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di gunung pada siang hari dan turun bersuka ria saat senja.",
        "en": "She dwells in the mountains by day and descends to revel at dusk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "acheri-c04",
      "source_id": "acheri-s1",
      "quote": "They are also said to bring disease to children (and sometimes others) who come in contact with their shadow (Cháyá).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bayangannya dipercaya menularkan penyakit kepada anak-anak.",
        "en": "Her shadow is believed to inflict disease on children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "acheri-c05",
      "source_id": "acheri-s1",
      "quote": "According to the separate accounts of William Crooke and Edwin Thomas Atkinson, one who wears a scarlet thread around their throat would be protected from colds and goitre.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan Crooke dan Atkinson mengaitkan benang merah di leher dengan perlindungan dari selesma dan gondok.",
        "en": "Crooke and Atkinson's accounts associate a scarlet neck thread with protection from colds and goitre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "acheri-c06",
      "source_id": "acheri-s1",
      "quote": "the majority of accounts point to red garments as only serving as a target for the Acheri's anger, as William Crooke claims them to have an antipathy to the color red.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Crooke justru menyebut pakaian merah memancing kemarahannya, berbeda dari benang pelindung.",
        "en": "Crooke instead calls red garments a target of her anger, distinct from the protective thread."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Apalala adalah raja naga penghuni air dalam mitologi Buddha, dikaitkan dengan Sungai Swat.",
    "en": "Apalala is a water dwelling naga king in Buddhist mythology associated with the Swat River.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "apalala-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Apalala adalah raja naga penghuni air dalam mitologi Buddha, dikaitkan dengan Sungai Swat. Legenda menuturkan Buddha mengubahnya menjadi penganut Buddha.",
      "en": "Apalala is a water dwelling naga king in Buddhist mythology associated with the Swat River. The legend recounts the Buddha converting him to Buddhism.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apalala-c01",
        "apalala-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah pertobatannya tampaknya tidak ada dalam Kanon Pali, meskipun namanya tercatat di antara makhluk yang menghormati Buddha. Samantapasadika menyebut kisah itu tidak dimasukkan dalam Tiga Konsili. Di Sri Lanka kisah itu termasuk adegan ruang relik Mahathupa. Divyavadana menempatkan pertobatannya tidak lama sebelum kematian Buddha.",
      "en": "His conversion story apparently is absent from the Pali Canon although his name appears among beings honoring the Buddha. The Samantapasadika says the story was excluded from the Three Councils. In Sri Lanka the story appears among scenes in the Mahathupa relic chamber. The Divyavadana places his conversion shortly before the Buddha's death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apalala-c03",
        "apalala-c04",
        "apalala-c05",
        "apalala-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    },
    {
      "id": "apalala-c03",
      "source_id": "apalala-s1",
      "quote": "The story of Apalāla's conversion (Pali: Apalāladamana) does not seem to be found in the Pali Canon, although his name does appear with other beings that honor the Buddha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah pertobatannya tampaknya tidak ada dalam Kanon Pali, meskipun namanya tercatat di antara makhluk yang menghormati Buddha.",
        "en": "His conversion story apparently is absent from the Pali Canon although his name appears among beings honoring the Buddha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalala-c04",
      "source_id": "apalala-s1",
      "quote": "The Samantapāsādikā mentions that this story was among those not included in the Three Councils.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Samantapasadika menyebut kisah itu tidak dimasukkan dalam Tiga Konsili.",
        "en": "The Samantapasadika says the story was excluded from the Three Councils."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalala-c05",
      "source_id": "apalala-s1",
      "quote": "it was known in Sri Lanka as it is mentioned among the scenes depicted in the relic-chamber of the Mahāthūpa.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Sri Lanka kisah itu termasuk adegan ruang relik Mahathupa.",
        "en": "In Sri Lanka the story appears among scenes in the Mahathupa relic chamber."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apalala-c06",
      "source_id": "apalala-s1",
      "quote": "The Divyāvadāna also mentions that Apalāla's conversion took place shortly before the Buddha's death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Divyavadana menempatkan pertobatannya tidak lama sebelum kematian Buddha.",
        "en": "The Divyavadana places his conversion shortly before the Buddha's death."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kalavinka adalah makhluk abadi Buddha berkepala manusia dan berbadan burung dengan ekor panjang.",
    "en": "Kalavinka is an immortal Buddhist creature with a human head, bird body, and long tail.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kalavinka-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kalavinka adalah makhluk abadi Buddha berkepala manusia dan berbadan burung dengan ekor panjang. Ia dipercaya tinggal di Tanah Murni Barat dan mengajarkan Dharma melalui suara indah.",
      "en": "Kalavinka is an immortal Buddhist creature with a human head, bird body, and long tail. It is believed to dwell in the Western Pure Land and teach the Dharma through a beautiful voice.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kalavinka-c01",
        "kalavinka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dikatakan bernyanyi ketika masih di dalam cangkang telur. Suaranya dipakai untuk menggambarkan suara Buddha. Schafer membedakannya dari kinnara meskipun seni keagamaan sering mencampuradukkan keduanya. Sumber Jepang menerangkan dua sosok kalavinka berpasangan pada hiasan keman emas Chusonji.",
      "en": "It is said to sing while still inside its eggshell. Its voice is used to describe the Buddha's voice. Schafer distinguishes it from the kinnara although religious art often confuses them. The Japanese source describes paired kalavinkas on a gilt keman ornament at Chusonji.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kalavinka-c03",
        "kalavinka-c04",
        "kalavinka-c05",
        "kalavinka-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,
  "abilities": [],

    {
      "id": "kalavinka-s1",

    {
      "id": "kalavinka-s2",

      "title": "迦陵頻伽",
      "author": null,

      "published": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kalavinka-c01",
      "source_id": "kalavinka-s1",
      "quote": "is a fantastical immortal creature in Buddhism, with a human head and a bird's torso, with long flowing tail.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kalavinka adalah makhluk abadi Buddha berkepala manusia dan berbadan burung dengan ekor panjang.",
        "en": "Kalavinka is an immortal Buddhist creature with a human head, bird body, and long tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kalavinka-c02",
      "source_id": "kalavinka-s1",
      "quote": "The kalaviṅka is said to dwell in the Western pure land and is reputed to preach the Dharma with its fine voice.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dipercaya tinggal di Tanah Murni Barat dan mengajarkan Dharma melalui suara indah.",
        "en": "It is believed to dwell in the Western Pure Land and teach the Dharma through a beautiful voice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kalavinka-c03",
      "source_id": "kalavinka-s1",
      "quote": "It is said to sing while still unhatched within its eggshell.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikatakan bernyanyi ketika masih di dalam cangkang telur.",
        "en": "It is said to sing while still inside its eggshell."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kalavinka-c04",
      "source_id": "kalavinka-s1",
      "quote": "Its voice is a descriptor of the Buddha's voice.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suaranya dipakai untuk menggambarkan suara Buddha.",
        "en": "Its voice is used to describe the Buddha's voice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kalavinka-c05",
      "source_id": "kalavinka-s1",
      "quote": "Edward H. Schafer notes that in East Asian religious art the Kalaviṅka is often confused with the Kinnara, which is also a half-human half-bird hybrid mythical creature, but that the two are actually distinct and unrelated.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Schafer membedakannya dari kinnara meskipun seni keagamaan sering mencampuradukkan keduanya.",
        "en": "Schafer distinguishes it from the kinnara although religious art often confuses them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kalavinka-c06",
      "source_id": "kalavinka-s2",
      "quote": "殻の中にいる時から鳴きだすとされる。その声は非常に美しく、仏の声を形容するのに用いられ、「妙音鳥」、「好声鳥」、「妙声鳥」、「逸音鳥」、「妙声鳥」、「好声」、「美音鳥」とも意訳される。『大般若経』、『楞厳呪』、『菩薩瓔珞本業経』などの仏典において仏の声の勝妙な様子を迦陵頻伽の鳴き声に譬える例がある。一説によれば、迦陵頻伽は現代のベンガル語においてブルブル (Bulbul) と呼ばれる雀の一種であるとされる。また、日本では美しい芸者や花魁、美声の芸妓を指してこの名で呼ぶこともあった。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sumber Jepang menerangkan dua sosok kalavinka berpasangan pada hiasan keman emas Chusonji.",
        "en": "The Japanese source describes paired kalavinkas on a gilt keman ornament at Chusonji."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Kumbhandas are a group of dwarfish misshapen spirits among lesser Buddhist deities.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "kumbhanda-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kumbhanda adalah kelompok roh bertubuh kecil dan tidak proporsional di antara dewa rendah Buddha. Namanya dikaitkan dengan labu dan perut besar yang menyerupai labu.",
      "en": "Kumbhandas are a group of dwarfish misshapen spirits among lesser Buddhist deities. Their name is associated with gourds and gourd like large bellies.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kumbhanda-c01",
        "kumbhanda-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Istilahnya kadang tumpang tindih dengan yaksha, istilah yang lebih umum. Mereka termasuk dewa Caturmaharajika dan tunduk kepada Virudhaka, penjaga selatan. Salah satu pemimpin mereka bernama Kumbhira. Dalam Atanatiya Sutta, Virulha memimpin kumbhanda yang menyanyi dan menari bersamanya.",
      "en": "The term sometimes overlaps with yaksha, a more general term. They belong to the Caturmaharajika deities under Virudhaka, guardian of the south. One of their chiefs is named Kumbhira. In the Atanatiya Sutta Virulha rules the kumbhandas who sing and dance with him.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kumbhanda-c01",
      "source_id": "kumbhanda-s1",
      "quote": "A kumbhāṇḍa (Sanskrit) or kumbhaṇḍa (Pāli) is one of a group of dwarfish, misshapen spirits among the lesser deities of Buddhist mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kumbhanda adalah kelompok roh bertubuh kecil dan tidak proporsional di antara dewa rendah Buddha.",
        "en": "Kumbhandas are a group of dwarfish misshapen spirits among lesser Buddhist deities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumbhanda-c02",
      "source_id": "kumbhanda-s1",
      "quote": "Kumbhāṇḍa was a dialectal form for \"gourd\", so they may get their name from being thought to resemble gourds in some way, e.g. in having big stomachs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya dikaitkan dengan labu dan perut besar yang menyerupai labu.",
        "en": "Their name is associated with gourds and gourd like large bellies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumbhanda-c03",
      "source_id": "kumbhanda-s1",
      "quote": "The terms kumbhāṇḍa and yakṣa are sometimes used for the same creature; yakṣa in these cases is the more general term, including a variety of lower deities.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istilahnya kadang tumpang tindih dengan yaksha, istilah yang lebih umum.",
        "en": "The term sometimes overlaps with yaksha, a more general term."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumbhanda-c04",
      "source_id": "kumbhanda-s1",
      "quote": "The kumbhāṇḍas are classed among the Cāturmahārājika deities, and are subject to the Great King Virūḍhaka, Guardian of the South. One of their chiefs is called Kumbhīra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka termasuk dewa Caturmaharajika dan tunduk kepada Virudhaka, penjaga selatan.",
        "en": "They belong to the Caturmaharajika deities under Virudhaka, guardian of the south."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumbhanda-c05",
      "source_id": "kumbhanda-s1",
      "quote": "The kumbhāṇḍas are classed among the Cāturmahārājika deities, and are subject to the Great King Virūḍhaka, Guardian of the South. One of their chiefs is called Kumbhīra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu pemimpin mereka bernama Kumbhira.",

      "id": "kumbhanda-c06",
      "source_id": "kumbhanda-s2",
      "quote": "a great king named Virulha who has a retinue of attendants, and is the sovereign lord of Kumbhandas. Attended by the Kumbhandas he enjoys their song and dance.",

    "en": "Lai Khutshangbi is a female hingchabi with very long hands in Meitei folklore of Manipur.",

  "long_description": [

      "id": "Lai Khutshangbi adalah hingchabi perempuan bertangan sangat panjang dalam cerita rakyat Meitei Manipur. Lai berarti dewa, khut berarti tangan, dan shangbi berarti panjang.",
      "en": "Lai Khutshangbi is a female hingchabi with very long hands in Meitei folklore of Manipur. Lai means deity, khut means hand, and shangbi means long.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lai-khutshangbi-c01",
        "lai-khutshangbi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tangannya menyentuh tanah saat berjalan, jarinya tajam seperti duri, dan lidahnya panjang. Ia tinggal di hutan dekat kampung dan mencuri serta memakan ternak dan bayi. Ia takut pada Shasipa, ayah anak yang ingin dimakannya. Shasipa memotong kedua tangannya; darah yang jatuh dipercaya menjadi bercak merah pada tanaman.",
      "en": "Her hands touch the ground as she walks, her fingers are thorn sharp, and her tongue is long. She lives in a forest near a village, stealing and eating livestock and babies.       "id": "lai-khutshangbi-c01",
      "source_id": "lai-khutshangbi-s1",
      "quote": "is a demoness (hingchabi) in Meitei mythology and folklore of Antique Kangleipak (Ancient Manipur). She has very long hands.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lai Khutshangbi adalah hingchabi perempuan bertangan sangat panjang dalam cerita rakyat Meitei Manipur.",
        "en": "Lai Khutshangbi is a female hingchabi with very long hands in Meitei folklore of Manipur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lai-khutshangbi-c02",
      "source_id": "lai-khutshangbi-s1",
      "quote": "The word \"Lai\" means \"deity or divinity,\" \"Khut\" means \"hand,\" and \"Sangbi\" or \"Shangbi\" means \"long\" in Manipuri (Meitei).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lai berarti dewa, khut berarti tangan, dan shangbi berarti panjang.",
        "en": "Lai means deity, khut means hand, and shangbi means long."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lai-khutshangbi-c03",
      "source_id": "lai-khutshangbi-s1",
      "quote": "The legend says that when she walks, her long hands touch the ground. Her fingers are as sharp as thorns. She has a large mouth with a long tongue.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tangannya menyentuh tanah saat berjalan, jarinya tajam seperti duri, dan lidahnya panjang.",
        "en": "Her hands touch the ground as she walks, her fingers are thorn sharp, and her tongue is long."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lai-khutshangbi-c04",
      "source_id": "lai-khutshangbi-s1",
      "quote": "There was a man and woman with a little child named Shasi (or \"Shachi\" or \"Leirik\" or \"Naocha\" in other versions) living in an isolated house in a hamlet. Nearby was the forest where Lai Khutsangbi lived. She usually stole and ate livestock and human babies from the village. She took advantage of people's loneliness, sowing terror in the people in the village.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia tinggal di hutan dekat kampung dan mencuri serta memakan ternak dan bayi.",
        "en": "She lives in a forest near a village, stealing and eating livestock and babies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lai-khutshangbi-c05",
      "source_id": "lai-khutshangbi-s1",
      "quote": "But Shasi's father, Shasipa was so brave that the Lai Khutsangbi was afraid of him.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia takut pada Shasipa, ayah anak yang ingin dimakannya.",
        "en": "She fears Shasipa, father of the child she wants to eat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lai-khutshangbi-c06",
      "source_id": "lai-khutshangbi-s1",
      "quote": "The blood flew out of her cut arm and fell onto many plants growing on her way to the forest. It is said that the red patches seen on some plants is her blood. ... Then, he chopped off that arm too.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shasipa memotong kedua tangannya; darah yang jatuh dipercaya menjadi bercak merah pada tanaman.",
        "en": "Shasipa cuts off both her arms; falling blood is believed to become red patches on plants."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

      "en": "Pancika is a yaksha and Hariti's consort. He is said to have five hundred children with Hariti.",

      "id": "Mahavamsa menyebutnya panglima pasukan yaksha Vaisravana dengan 27 jenderal di bawahnya.       "source_id": "pancika-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Mahavamsa, Pañcika was the commander-in-chief of the yakṣa army of Vaiśravaṇa and had another 27 yakṣa generals under his orders.",

      "source_id": "pancika-s1",

      "en": "Bramrachokh is a Kashmiri creature inhabiting desolate places and misleading travelers with light. He is also called Bramaracokh or Rachok.",

        "bramrachokh-c02"

    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membawa periuk penuh api di atas kepala. Di dahinya terdapat mata yang bersinar terang. Cahayanya dikatakan menuntun pelancong ke parit, gua, atau kematian. Anak desa kadang mengaitkan cahaya berkedip di kejauhan dengan dirinya.",
