      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-s2",
      "url": "https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/13325/pg13325-images.html",
      "title": "SKETCHES OF THE NATURAL HISTORY OF CEYLON",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Longman, Green, Longman, and Roberts; Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "folklore-collection",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-c01",
      "source_id": "devil-bird-s1",
      "quote": "In Sri Lankan folklore, the Devil Bird or Ulama ... is a creature said to emit bloodcurdling human-sounding shrieks in jungles at night.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Devil Bird atau Ulama adalah burung legenda Sri Lanka dengan jeritan menyerupai suara manusia pada malam hari.",
        "en": "The Devil Bird or Ulama is a Sri Lankan legendary bird with human sounding night shrieks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-c02",
      "source_id": "devil-bird-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed that the cry of this bird is an omen that portends death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tangisannya dipercaya menjadi pertanda kematian.",
        "en": "Its cry is believed to portend death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-c03",
      "source_id": "devil-bird-s1",
      "quote": "Its precise identity is still a matter of debate",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Identitas zoologisnya masih diperdebatkan.",
        "en": "Its zoological identity remains disputed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-c04",
      "source_id": "devil-bird-s1",
      "quote": "Other possible identities include the crested honey buzzard (Pernis ptilorhynchus ruficollis), the Brown wood owl (Strix leptogrammica) and various eagles.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Burung kandidat mencakup honey buzzard, brown wood owl, dan berbagai elang.",
        "en": "Candidate birds include the honey buzzard, brown wood owl, and various eagles."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-c05",
      "source_id": "devil-bird-s2",
      "quote": "On her death she was metamorphosed, according to the\r\nBuddhist belief, into an ulama, or Devil-bird, which still\r\nat nightfall horrifies the villagers by repeating the frantic\r\nscreams of the bereaved mother in her agony.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 836",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tennent menuturkan versi ibu yang berubah menjadi ulama setelah kehilangan anaknya dan kemudian menjerit pada malam hari.",
        "en": "Tennent recounts a version of a mother becoming an ulama after losing her child and then shrieking at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devil-bird-c06",
      "source_id": "devil-bird-s2",
      "quote": "The Devil-Bird is not an owl. ... I have offered rewards for a specimen,\r\nbut without success.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 931",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tennent mengutip Mitford yang menolak identifikasi sebagai burung hantu dan mengaku belum mendapatkan spesimen.",
        "en": "Tennent quotes Mitford rejecting an owl identification and admitting he has not obtained a specimen."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Ilustrasi dalam artikel tidak dipakai tanpa pemeriksaan berkas Commons, kecocokan subjek, dan lisensinya; belum ada gambar yang terverifikasi untuk entri ini."

}

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mada dalam Mahabharata adalah kegilaan yang dipersonifikasikan sebagai asura. Ia termasuk krtya, makhluk hasil sihir.",
      "en": "Mada in the Mahabharata is madness personified as an asura. He is categorized as a krtya, a product of sorcery.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mada-c01",
        "mada-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia digambarkan amat besar dan mampu menelan seluruh kosmos. Cyavana memanggilnya ketika Indra menolak memberi Ashvin hak meminum soma. Ia muncul dari api persembahan. Setelah Indra menyerah, Cyavana menariknya kembali dan membaginya menjadi empat bagian.",
      "en": "He is described as immense and able to swallow the entire cosmos. Cyavana calls him when Indra refuses the Ashvins the right to drink soma. He appears from the sacrificial fire. After Indra surrenders Cyavana withdraws him and divides him into four parts.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mada-c03",
        "mada-c04",
        "mada-c05",
        "mada-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",

      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "mada-c02",
      "source_id": "mada-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia termasuk krtya, makhluk hasil sihir.",
        "en": "He is categorized as a krtya, a product of sorcery."

    {

      "source_id": "mada-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan amat besar dan mampu menelan seluruh kosmos.",
        "en": "He is described as immense and able to swallow the entire cosmos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mada-c04",
      "source_id": "mada-s1",
      "quote": "The Asvinidevas wanted the ban Indra had placed on drinking soma lifted. ... Indra objected to the Asvinidevas partaking in the soma and began a fight with Cyavana. At that time, Cyavana calls upon Mada,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",

        "en": "Cyavana calls him when Indra refuses the Ashvins the right to drink soma."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mada-c05",
      "source_id": "mada-s1",
      "quote": "Mada, who appears out of the sacrificial fire.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "He appears from the sacrificial fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mada-c06",
      "source_id": "mada-s1",
      "quote": "Once Indra surrenders, Cyavana withdraws Mada and tears him into four pieces.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah Indra menyerah, Cyavana menariknya kembali dan membaginya menjadi empat bagian.",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Manussiha adalah makhluk mitos Burma berbentuk setengah manusia dan setengah singa. Legenda mengaitkan penciptaannya oleh biksu dengan perlindungan bayi kerajaan dari raksasi laut.",
      "en": "Manussiha is a Burmese mythical creature half human and half lion. The legend associates its creation by monks with protecting a royal baby from sea ogresses.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manussiha-c01",
        "manussiha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Patungnya biasanya menjaga keempat sudut pagoda. Ia memiliki kepala dan torso manusia serta bagian belakang singa. Nama Pali-nya menggabungkan manussa, manusia, dan siha, singa. Sumber Prancis menghubungkan manussiha dengan kisah biksu yang melindungi bayi kerajaan dari raksasi.",
      "en": "Its statues usually guard the four corners of a pagoda. It has a human head and torso with lion hindquarters. Its Pali name combines manussa, human, and siha, lion. The French source links the manussiha to a tale of monks protecting a royal baby from a rakshasi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "manussiha-c03",
        "manussiha-c04",
        "manussiha-c05",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manussiha-c02",
      "source_id": "manussiha-s1",
      "quote": "believed to be created by Buddhist missionary monks to protect a new-born royal baby from being devoured by rakshasis (ogresses) from the sea.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manussiha-c03",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manussiha-c04",
      "source_id": "manussiha-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memiliki kepala dan torso manusia serta bagian belakang singa.",
        "en": "It has a human head and torso with lion hindquarters."
      }

      "id": "manussiha-c05",
      "source_id": "manussiha-s1",
      "quote": "Manussīha(မနုဿီဟ) is a combination of two Pali words; Manussa(မနုဿ) meaning \"human\" and Sīha(သီဟ) meaning \"lion\". Thus, it can be literally translated as \"Man-lion\".",

    {
      "id": "manussiha-c06",
      "source_id": "manussiha-s2",
      "quote": "En 235 ans après Parinibbāna de Bouddha, le missionnaire bouddhiste de cinq mathērs (moines seniors) dirigé par Soṇa et Uttara est arrivé à Sudhammapura de Suvaṇṇabhūmi où le roi Sirīmāsoka régnait à cette époque. Comme la ville est près de la mer, les bīlūḥmas (ogresses) vivant dans la mer venaient manger les bébés des maisons royales.",

    "id": "Naale Ba adalah legenda roh jahat yang berkeliaran malam hari di Karnataka, India.",
    "en": "Naale Ba is a legend of a malevolent spirit roaming at night in Karnataka, India.",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naale Ba adalah legenda roh jahat yang berkeliaran malam hari di Karnataka, India. Orang menuliskan frasa itu di pintu atau dinding untuk mencegah roh masuk.",
      "en": "Naale Ba is a legend of a malevolent spirit roaming at night in Karnataka, India. People write the phrase on doors or walls to prevent the spirit entering.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "naale-ba-c01",
        "naale-ba-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam satu versi ia mengetuk pintu dan meniru suara kerabat saat memanggil penghuni. Versi lain menyebut hantu perempuan yang mencari suaminya dan membawa pergi lelaki penghuni rumah. Tulisan datang besok dipercaya mengusirnya dengan menunda kunjungan terus-menerus. Liputan Yerasala mencatat tulisan Naale Ba berarti datang besok, digunakan warga sebagai penolak roh pada 1990-an.",
      "en": "In one version it knocks on doors and imitates relatives when calling residents. Another version describes a female ghost seeking her husband and taking away a male resident. The come tomorrow sign is believed to repel it by continually postponing its visit. Yerasala's report records Naale Ba meaning come tomorrow, used by residents to ward off a spirit in the 1990s.",

      ]

      "type": "news",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "naale-ba-c01",
      "source_id": "naale-ba-s1",
      "quote": "is a popular folk legend which features prominently in areas across Karnataka, India. ... It is believed that a malevolent spirit or witch roams around at night",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naale Ba adalah legenda roh jahat yang berkeliaran malam hari di Karnataka, India.",
        "en": "Naale Ba is a legend of a malevolent spirit roaming at night in Karnataka, India."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naale-ba-c02",
      "source_id": "naale-ba-s1",
      "quote": "People write this on walls to prevent the entry of a malevolent spirit into their homes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang menuliskan frasa itu di pintu atau dinding untuk mencegah roh masuk.",
        "en": "People write the phrase on doors or walls to prevent the spirit entering."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naale-ba-c03",
      "source_id": "naale-ba-s1",
      "quote": "In some variations, people believed that a demon roams around, knocks on doors and calls out names of people imitating the voice of their kin.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu versi ia mengetuk pintu dan meniru suara kerabat saat memanggil penghuni.",
        "en": "In one version it knocks on doors and imitates relatives when calling residents."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naale-ba-c04",
      "source_id": "naale-ba-s1",
      "quote": "There are also variations of the story where the spirit is believed to be a female ghost in search of her husband. She is known to take away the man of the house,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menyebut hantu perempuan yang mencari suaminya dan membawa pergi lelaki penghuni rumah.",
        "en": "Another version describes a female ghost seeking her husband and taking away a male resident."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naale-ba-c05",
      "source_id": "naale-ba-s1",
      "quote": "It was believed that when the ghost read this sign, it goes back to come again the next day, only for the cycle to keep repeating.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tulisan datang besok dipercaya mengusirnya dengan menunda kunjungan terus-menerus.",
        "en": "The come tomorrow sign is believed to repel it by continually postponing its visit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naale-ba-c06",
      "source_id": "naale-ba-s2",
      "quote": "the talk of the town in the 90s, ... Naale Baa (come tomorrow) ... to deter a witch/an evil spirit",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Liputan Yerasala mencatat tulisan Naale Ba berarti datang besok, digunakan warga sebagai penolak roh pada 1990-an.",
        "en": "Yerasala's report records Naale Ba meaning come tomorrow, used by residents to ward off a spirit in the 1990s."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    {

    "en": "The Nagas of Sri Lankan legend are a class of superhuman serpent beings in a subterranean world.",

    {
      "id": "Naga dalam legenda Sri Lanka adalah kelompok makhluk supermanusia berwujud ular di dunia bawah tanah. Mereka disebut dalam Mahavamsa dan Manimekala.",
      "en": "The Nagas of Sri Lankan legend are a class of superhuman serpent beings in a subterranean world. They are mentioned in the Mahavamsa and Manimekala.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "naga-people-c01",
        "naga-people-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nagadeepa dan Kalyani disebut sebagai tempat tinggal mereka. Buddha mendamaikan raja naga Chulodara dan Mahodara yang bersengketa tentang takhta berpermata. Maniakkhita, raja naga Kalyani, mengundang Buddha untuk kunjungan ketiganya ke pulau. Terjemahan Geiger menyebut Mahodara sebagai raja berkekuatan ajaib di kerajaan naga di samudra.",
      "en": "Nagadeepa and Kalyani are mentioned as their abodes. The Buddha reconciles naga kings Chulodara and Mahodara disputing a jeweled throne. Maniakkhita, naga king of Kalyani, invites the Buddha for his third visit to the island. Geiger's translation calls Mahodara a king with miraculous power in an ocean naga kingdom.",
      "claim_ids": [

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "naga-people-c01",
      "source_id": "naga-people-s1",
      "quote": "They were generally represented as a class of super-humans taking the form of serpents who inhabit a subterranean world.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga dalam legenda Sri Lanka adalah kelompok makhluk supermanusia berwujud ular di dunia bawah tanah.",
        "en": "The Nagas of Sri Lankan legend are a class of superhuman serpent beings in a subterranean world."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-people-c02",
      "source_id": "naga-people-s1",
      "quote": "There are references to Nagas in several ancient texts such as Mahavamsa, Manimekala.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka disebut dalam Mahavamsa dan Manimekala.",
        "en": "They are mentioned in the Mahavamsa and Manimekala."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-people-c03",
      "source_id": "naga-people-s1",
      "quote": "Certain places such as Nagadeepa in Jaffna and Kalyani in Gampaha are mentioned as their abodes. The names of some Naga kings in Sri Lankan Ancient legend and the cult of Naga prevailed in India up to medieval times.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nagadeepa dan Kalyani disebut sebagai tempat tinggal mereka.",
        "en": "Nagadeepa and Kalyani are mentioned as their abodes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-people-c04",
      "source_id": "naga-people-s1",
      "quote": "The chronicle states that the Buddha, during his second visit to the island, pacified a dispute between two Naga Kings of Nagadeepa, Chulodara and Mahodara over the possession of a gem-studded throne.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buddha mendamaikan raja naga Chulodara dan Mahodara yang bersengketa tentang takhta berpermata.",
        "en": "The Buddha reconciles naga kings Chulodara and Mahodara disputing a jeweled throne."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-people-c05",
      "source_id": "naga-people-s1",
      "quote": "the Buddha visited the island for the third time, on an invitation of Maniakkhita, the Naga king of Kalyani",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Maniakkhita, raja naga Kalyani, mengundang Buddha untuk kunjungan ketiganya ke pulau.",
        "en": "Maniakkhita, naga king of Kalyani, invites the Buddha for his third visit to the island."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-people-c06",
      "source_id": "naga-people-s2",
      "quote": "That same naga Mahodara was then a king, gifted with \nmiraculous power, in a naga-kingdom in the ocean,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Terjemahan Geiger menyebut Mahodara sebagai raja berkekuatan ajaib di kerajaan naga di samudra.",
        "en": "Geiger's translation calls Mahodara a king with miraculous power in an ocean naga kingdom."
      }
    }

      "id": "Nongshaba adalah dewa singa dalam Sanamahism dan mitologi Meitei. Ia dipandang sebagai raja para dewa.",
      "en": "Nongshaba is a lion god in Sanamahism and Meitei mythology. He is regarded as king of the gods.",

        "nongshaba-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dipercaya menghasilkan cahaya primordial dan membuat matahari. Ia dipuja oleh masyarakat klan Ningthouja dan Moirang. Di Moirang ia menjadi dewa garis keturunan dan ayah Thangching. Kangla Sha dianggap representasi ilahinya.",

      "source_id": "ahiravana-s1",
      "quote": "In some versions of the Ramayana such as the Krittivasi Ramayana, ahiravana, is the brother of the rakshasa king Ravana.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam beberapa versi Ramayana, Ahiravana adalah saudara raja raksasa Ravana.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahiravana-c02",
      "source_id": "ahiravana-s1",
      "quote": "He secretly carried away Rama and his brother Lakshmana to the nether-world,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa Rama dan Lakshmana ke dunia bawah tanah secara diam-diam.",
        "en": "He secretly carries Rama and Lakshmana to the netherworld."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahiravana-c03",
      "source_id": "ahiravana-s1",
      "quote": "decided to sacrifice the life of the two divine brothers at the altar of his chosen deity, goddess Mahamaya.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merencanakan pengorbanan kedua saudara itu bagi Mahamaya.",
        "en": "He plans to sacrifice the brothers to Mahamaya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahiravana-c04",
      "source_id": "ahiravana-s1",
      "quote": "However, Hanuman saved their lives by decapitating ahiravana, and destroying his army.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

    },
    {
      "id": "ahiravana-c05",
      "source_id": "ahiravana-s1",
      "quote": "One night, Mahiravana, using his maya, took Vibhishana's form and entered Rama's camp.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam penuturan Mahiravana, ia memasuki perkemahan dengan menyamar sebagai Vibhishana.",
        "en": "In the Mahiravana account he enters the camp disguised as Vibhishana."

    },
    {
      "id": "ahiravana-c06",
      "source_id": "ahiravana-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",

      "statement": {

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk lainnya disebut gajasiha.",
        "en": "It is also called gajasiha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gajasimha-c03",
      "source_id": "gajasimha-s1",
      "quote": "The gajasimha or gajasiha (from Sanskrit: gaja+siṃha / Pali: gaja+sīha)",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya disusun dari gaja dan simha dalam Sanskerta, atau gaja dan siha dalam Pali.",
        "en": "Its name combines Sanskrit gaja and simha, or Pali gaja and siha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gajasimha-c04",
      "source_id": "gajasimha-s1",
      "quote": "It is found as a motif in Indian and Sinhalese art, and is used as a heraldic symbol in some Southeast Asian countries, especially Cambodia and Thailand.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Figurnya menjadi motif seni India dan Sinhala serta lambang di Asia Tenggara.",
        "en": "Its figure is a motif in Indian and Sinhalese art and a symbol in Southeast Asia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gajasimha-c05",
      "source_id": "gajasimha-s1",
      "quote": "In Siam (pre-modern Thailand), the gajasimha served as the symbol of the kalahom, one of the king's two chief chancellors.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Siam ia melambangkan kalahom, salah satu dua kanselir utama raja.",
        "en": "In Siam it symbolizes the kalahom, one of the king's two chief chancellors."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gajasimha-c06",
      "source_id": "gajasimha-s1",
      "quote": "It appears as a supporter in the coat of arms of Siam, in use from 1873 to 1910,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi penopang dalam lambang Siam yang digunakan pada 1873–1910.",
        "en": "It is a supporter in Siam's coat of arms used in 1873–1910."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Guhyaka adalah golongan makhluk supernatural Hindu yang melayani Kubera dan menjaga kekayaan tersembunyinya.",
    "en": "Guhyakas are a class of Hindu supernatural beings serving Kubera and guarding his hidden wealth.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "guhyaka-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Guhyaka adalah golongan makhluk supernatural Hindu yang melayani Kubera dan menjaga kekayaan tersembunyinya. Nama mereka berarti yang tersembunyi, dikaitkan dengan tempat tinggal di gua gunung.",
      "en": "Guhyakas are a class of Hindu supernatural beings serving Kubera and guarding his hidden wealth. Their name means hidden ones and is associated with dwelling in mountain caves.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "guhyaka-c01",
        "guhyaka-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hopkins menafsirkan mereka mungkin sebutan umum roh penyembunyian, sedangkan sebagian teks membedakannya dari yaksha. Mereka menjalankan tugas penting dan menjadi utusan Kubera. Markandeya Purana menuturkan Surya menetapkan Revanta sebagai pemimpin guhyaka. Dalam doa Yudhishthira kepada matahari, guhyaka bersama makhluk lain mengikuti kereta dewa untuk mencari anugerah.",
      "en": "Hopkins interprets them as possibly a general term for spirits of concealment while some texts distinguish them from yakshas. They carry out important tasks and act as Kubera's messengers. The Markandeya Purana recounts Surya appointing Revanta as chief of the guhyakas. In Yudhishthira's prayer to the sun guhyakas follow the deity's chariot with other beings seeking boons.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "guhyaka-c03",
        "guhyaka-c04",
        "guhyaka-c05",
        "guhyaka-c06"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "guhyaka-c01",
      "source_id": "guhyaka-s1",
      "quote": "is a class of supernatural beings in Hindu mythology. ... they are often described as attendants of Kubera, the keeper of wealth, and protect his hidden treasures.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Guhyaka adalah golongan makhluk supernatural Hindu yang melayani Kubera dan menjaga kekayaan tersembunyinya.",
        "en": "Guhyakas are a class of Hindu supernatural beings serving Kubera and guarding his hidden wealth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guhyaka-c02",
      "source_id": "guhyaka-s1",
      "quote": "Guhyaka(s) (गुह्यक, literally \"hidden ones\") ... Guhyakas are believed to live in mountain caves; thus their name, \"hidden ones\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama mereka berarti yang tersembunyi, dikaitkan dengan tempat tinggal di gua gunung.",
        "en": "Their name means hidden ones and is associated with dwelling in mountain caves."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guhyaka-c03",
      "source_id": "guhyaka-s1",
      "quote": "Edward Washburn Hopkins suggests that Guhyakas may not be distinct beings, but a generic name for spirits of concealment. They are mentioned in the Manu Smriti and the Harivamsa, appendix of the epic Mahabharata, as distinct beings; however, the epic and the Meghaduta identify them with Yakshas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hopkins menafsirkan mereka mungkin sebutan umum roh penyembunyian, sedangkan sebagian teks membedakannya dari yaksha.",
        "en": "Hopkins interprets them as possibly a general term for spirits of concealment while some texts distinguish them from yakshas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guhyaka-c04",
      "source_id": "guhyaka-s1",
      "quote": "Kubera assigns them important tasks ... They also serve as messengers and are sent to witness battles.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menjalankan tugas penting dan menjadi utusan Kubera.",
        "en": "They carry out important tasks and act as Kubera's messengers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guhyaka-c05",
      "source_id": "guhyaka-s1",
      "quote": "The Markandeya Purana mentions that Revanta was assigned the duty as the chief of Guhyakas by Surya.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Markandeya Purana menuturkan Surya menetapkan Revanta sebagai pemimpin guhyaka.",
        "en": "The Markandeya Purana recounts Surya appointing Revanta as chief of the guhyakas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "guhyaka-c06",
      "source_id": "guhyaka-s2",
      "quote": "The Siddhas, and the Charanas and the\r\nGandharvas and the Yakshas, and the Guhyakas,\r\nand the Nagas, desirous of obtaining boons follow thy car\r\ncoursing through the skies.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 24",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam doa Yudhishthira kepada matahari, guhyaka bersama makhluk lain mengikuti kereta dewa untuk mencari anugerah.",
        "en": "In Yudhishthira's prayer to the sun guhyakas follow the deity's chariot with other beings seeking boons."
      }
    }

    "id": "Gurumapa adalah makhluk legenda Nepal Mandala yang membawa pergi anak tidak patuh.",
    "en": "Gurumapa is a legendary Nepal Mandala creature taking away disobedient children.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gurumapa-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gurumapa adalah makhluk legenda Nepal Mandala yang membawa pergi anak tidak patuh. Ia digambarkan sebagai raksasa berwajah menakutkan dan bertaring menonjol dalam cerita Newar.",
      "en": "Gurumapa is a legendary Nepal Mandala creature taking away disobedient children. He is depicted as a giant with a frightening face and protruding fangs in Newar tales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gurumapa-c01",
        "gurumapa-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia adalah raksasa pemakan manusia di hutan yang mendekati Kesh Chandra setelah mencium mangsa. Kesh Chandra membujuknya membawa emas ke rumah dengan janji pesta dan hak mengambil anak yang diancam orang tua. Warga membujuknya pindah ke Tundikhel dengan janji persembahan tahunan nasi rebus dan daging kerbau. Nepali Times melaporkan empat lempeng tembaga Itumbaha menuturkan legenda Gurumapa dan pendirian biara.",
      "en": "He is a forest man eating giant approaching Kesh Chandra after smelling prey. Kesh Chandra persuades him to carry gold home with a feast and the right to take children threatened by parents. Residents persuade him to move to Tundikhel with annual offerings of boiled rice and buffalo meat. Nepali Times reports that four copper plates at Itumbaha tell Gurumapa's legend and the monastery's establishment.",

        "gurumapa-c03",
        "gurumapa-c04",
        "gurumapa-c05",
        "gurumapa-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "is a mythical creature in the folklore of Nepal Mandala. According to legend, he is said to take away disobedient children,",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gurumapa-c02",
      "source_id": "gurumapa-s1",
      "quote": "The story of Gurumapa is one of the most well known folk tales in Newar society. He is depicted as a giant with a terrifying face and protruding fangs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      }

    {
      "id": "gurumapa-c03",
      "source_id": "gurumapa-s1",
      "quote": "he saw Gurumapa, a man-eating giant that lived in the forest, approaching. He had been attracted by the smell of prey.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah raksasa pemakan manusia di hutan yang mendekati Kesh Chandra setelah mencium mangsa.",
        "en": "He is a forest man eating giant approaching Kesh Chandra after smelling prey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gurumapa-c04",
      "source_id": "gurumapa-s1",
      "quote": "Kesh Chandran pacified him by calling him uncle, and persuaded him to carry the gold to his home with the promise of a feast and the right to take away children if their parents called him whenever they were bad.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gurumapa-c05",
      "source_id": "gurumapa-s1",
      "quote": "They promised to provide him an annual feast of boiled rice and buffalo meat if he agreed to live on the field of Tinkhya (Tundikhel).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Warga membujuknya pindah ke Tundikhel dengan janji persembahan tahunan nasi rebus dan daging kerbau.",
        "en": "Residents persuade him to move to Tundikhel with annual offerings of boiled rice and buffalo meat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gurumapa-c06",
      "source_id": "gurumapa-s2",
      "quote": "In the monastery's north wing are four copper plates that tell the legend of Gurumapa and the establishment of Itumbaha.",

      "id": "Hydaspes adalah dewa sungai dalam mitologi Yunani yang dikaitkan dengan Jhelum. Ia merupakan putra Thaumas dan Elektra, seorang Oceanid.",
      "en": "Hydaspes is a river god in Greek mythology associated with the Jhelum. He is the son of Thaumas and Elektra, an Oceanid.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hydaspes-c01",
        "hydaspes-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Iris adalah saudara perempuannya. Bersama Astris, putri Helios, ia menjadi ayah Deriades. Dionysiaca Nonnus menuturkan dukungannya bagi penduduk dalam perang melawan Dionysos. Dalam kutipan Nonnus, Hydaspes menyelamatkan putranya dari kematian.",
      "en": "Iris is his sister. With Astris, Helios's daughter, he fathers Deriades. Nonnus's Dionysiaca recounts his support for the inhabitants in their war against Dionysos. In the Nonnus excerpt Hydaspes rescues his stricken son from death.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hydaspes-c01",
      "source_id": "hydaspes-s1",
      "quote": "was a river god of Pentapotamia ... It is the modern day Jhelum River",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hydaspes adalah dewa sungai dalam mitologi Yunani yang dikaitkan dengan Jhelum.",
        "en": "Hydaspes is a river god in Greek mythology associated with the Jhelum."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydaspes-c02",
      "source_id": "hydaspes-s1",
      "quote": "Hydaspes was a Titan-descended god, the son of the sea-god Thaumas and the cloud-goddess Elektra, an Oceanid.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan putra Thaumas dan Elektra, seorang Oceanid.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydaspes-c03",
      "source_id": "hydaspes-s1",
      "quote": "He was the brother of Iris, the messenger goddess of the rainbow.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iris adalah saudara perempuannya.",
        "en": "Iris is his sister."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydaspes-c04",
      "source_id": "hydaspes-s1",
      "quote": "By the Heliad Astris, the daughter of Helios and the Oceanid Ceto, Hydaspes fathered Deriades the king of Pentapotamia (Punjab).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bersama Astris, putri Helios, ia menjadi ayah Deriades.",
        "en": "With Astris, Helios's daughter, he fathers Deriades."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydaspes-c05",
      "source_id": "hydaspes-s1",
      "quote": "The poet Nonnus in his Dionysiaca mentioned Hydaspes supported the natives in their war against the invading armies of the god Dionysos.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Nonnus's Dionysiaca recounts his support for the inhabitants in their war against Dionysos."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hydaspes-c06",
      "source_id": "hydaspes-s2",
      "quote": "but Hydaspes rescued his\n        stricken son from death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 14",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kutipan Nonnus, Hydaspes menyelamatkan putranya dari kematian.",
        "en": "In the Nonnus excerpt Hydaspes rescues his stricken son from death."
      }
    }

    },
    {
      "id": "jatasura-c02",
      "source_id": "jatasura-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Mahabharata, Jatasura appeared in the guise of a Brahmin to the Pandavas. His goal was to gain their confidence",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menyamar sebagai brahmana untuk mendapatkan kepercayaan Pandawa.",
        "en": "He poses as a Brahmin to gain the Pandavas' trust."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatasura-c03",
      "source_id": "jatasura-s1",
      "quote": "One day, when Bhima was away, Jatasura assumed a monstrous form. He seized the weapons and also abducted Yudhishthira, Sahadeva, Nakula, and Draupadi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Saat Bhima pergi, ia mengambil wujud monster serta merampas senjata dan menculik Yudhishthira, Sahadeva, Nakula, dan Draupadi.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatasura-c04",
      "source_id": "jatasura-s1",
      "quote": "Sahadeva managed to escape from his grasp, and rushed to seek the aid of Bhima.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sahadeva lolos dan mencari bantuan Bhima.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatasura-c05",
      "source_id": "jatasura-s1",
      "quote": "Yudhishthira confused their captor by showering him with moral accusations, observing Jatasura's lack of intelligence.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yudhishthira membingungkannya dengan tuduhan moral.",
        "en": "Yudhishthira confuses him with moral accusations."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jatasura-c06",
      "source_id": "jatasura-s1",
      "quote": "The Pandava and the rakshasa fought with gigantic trees, large rocks, along with their arms. ... In the end, Bhima was able to inflict a death blow on his opponent's neck.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melawan Bhima menggunakan pohon besar, batu, dan tangan sebelum dikalahkan.",

    "en": "Taoroinai is a snake like dragon in Meitei mythology and Manipur's Sanamahism dwelling in the land of the Moon.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "taoroinai-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Taoroinai adalah naga menyerupai ular dalam mitologi Meitei dan Sanamahism Manipur yang tinggal di negeri Bulan. Shakok Lamlen menyebut Kangla dibangun di atas pusarnya.",
      "en": "Taoroinai is a snake like dragon in Meitei mythology and Manipur's Sanamahism dwelling in the land of the Moon. The Shakok Lamlen says Kangla was built over his navel.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taoroinai-c01",
        "taoroinai-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sanggai Phammang menuturkan ia membawa citra Atiya ke bumi dalam mulut, kemudian citra itu lahir sebagai anak ilahi. Leithak Leikharol dan Krathok Lamlen menuturkan penyamaran sebagai Tupu untuk membawa telur awan Atiya yang kemudian menjadi Pakhangba. Leimaren Naoyom menuturkan ia memberikan telur embrio kepada Leimarel Sidabi yang kemudian melahirkan Pakhangba. Anoirol menuturkan ia mengajari putrinya Toibi Tanka Nubi menari.",
      "en": "The Sanggai Phammang recounts him carrying Atiya's image to earth in his mouth, the image later being born as a divine boy. The Leithak Leikharol and Krathok Lamlen recount a Tupu disguise to bring Atiya's cloud egg, later becoming Pakhangba. The Leimaren Naoyom recounts him giving an embryonic egg to Leimarel Sidabi, who later bears Pakhangba. The Anoirol recounts him teaching his daughter Toibi Tanka Nubi to dance.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "taoroinai-c03",
        "taoroinai-c04",
        "taoroinai-c05",
        "taoroinai-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    "id": "Teks berbeda memberikan versi citra ilahi atau telur awan yang dibawa naga; versi itu tidak digabung menjadi satu urutan kejadian.",

      "publisher": "Wikipedia",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "taoroinai-c01",
      "source_id": "taoroinai-s1",
      "quote": "is a snake-like dragon in Meitei mythology and Sanamahism, the indigenous religion of Manipur. It lived in the land of the Moon.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taoroinai adalah naga menyerupai ular dalam mitologi Meitei dan Sanamahism Manipur yang tinggal di negeri Bulan.",
        "en": "Taoroinai is a snake like dragon in Meitei mythology and Manipur's Sanamahism dwelling in the land of the Moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taoroinai-c02",
      "source_id": "taoroinai-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Shakok Lamlen, the Kangla was constructed over the navel of Taoroinai.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shakok Lamlen menyebut Kangla dibangun di atas pusarnya.",
        "en": "The Shakok Lamlen says Kangla was built over his navel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taoroinai-c03",
      "source_id": "taoroinai-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Sanggai Phammang, Taoroinai lived on the Moon. He was ordered by the God Atiya (Atingkok) to carry His image to the Earth. He swallowed the image of the God in his mouth and brought it down. He lived inside the Earth. The God's image was later born as a divine boy.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sanggai Phammang menuturkan ia membawa citra Atiya ke bumi dalam mulut, kemudian citra itu lahir sebagai anak ilahi.",
        "en": "The Sanggai Phammang recounts him carrying Atiya's image to earth in his mouth, the image later being born as a divine boy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taoroinai-c04",
      "source_id": "taoroinai-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Leithak Leikharol and the Krathok Lamlen, Taoroinai went to the heavens disguises as Tupu (officer in charge) and brought down the divine cloud egg (nonglum) of Atiya. He gave the egg to a polyandrous person. Later, the egg became Pakhangba.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Leithak Leikharol dan Krathok Lamlen menuturkan penyamaran sebagai Tupu untuk membawa telur awan Atiya yang kemudian menjadi Pakhangba.",
        "en": "The Leithak Leikharol and Krathok Lamlen recount a Tupu disguise to bring Atiya's cloud egg, later becoming Pakhangba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taoroinai-c05",
      "source_id": "taoroinai-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Leimaren Naoyom, Taoroinai gave an embryonic egg to Leimarel Sidabi, a solar goddess (or a celestial goddess). Later, goddess Leimaren (alias Yaibirok) gave birth to Pakhangba.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Leimaren Naoyom menuturkan ia memberikan telur embrio kepada Leimarel Sidabi yang kemudian melahirkan Pakhangba.",
        "en": "The Leimaren Naoyom recounts him giving an embryonic egg to Leimarel Sidabi, who later bears Pakhangba."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "taoroinai-c06",
      "source_id": "taoroinai-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Anoirol, Lady Toibi Tanka Nubi (Tankha Chanu) danced with her father, Taoroinai. She learned how to dance from Taoroinai step by step and movement by movement.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Anoirol menuturkan ia mengajari putrinya Toibi Tanka Nubi menari.",
        "en": "The Anoirol recounts him teaching his daughter Toibi Tanka Nubi to dance."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Dua Belas Jenderal Langit adalah dewa pelindung atau yaksha Bhaisajyaguru, Buddha penyembuhan, dalam Buddha Asia Timur.",
    "en": "The Twelve Heavenly Generals are protective deities or yakshas of Bhaisajyaguru, the healing Buddha, in East Asian Buddhism.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dua Belas Jenderal Langit adalah dewa pelindung atau yaksha Bhaisajyaguru, Buddha penyembuhan, dalam Buddha Asia Timur. Mereka diperkenalkan dalam Medicine Buddha Sutra.",
      "en": "The Twelve Heavenly Generals are protective deities or yakshas of Bhaisajyaguru, the healing Buddha, in East Asian Buddhism. They are introduced in the Medicine Buddha Sutra.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "twelve-heavenly-generals-c01",
        "twelve-heavenly-generals-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama tepat mereka dapat berbeda menurut tradisi. Pemetaan zodiak dan honji juga bervariasi menurut teks dan daerah. Dalam tradisi Jepang mereka menjaga dua belas waktu, bulan, atau arah sesuai dua belas ikrar Buddha penyembuhan. Setiap jenderal memimpin tujuh ribu yaksha, total delapan puluh empat ribu.",
      "en": "Their precise names can differ by tradition. Their zodiac and honji correspondences also vary by text and region. In Japanese tradition they guard twelve times, months, or directions corresponding to the healing Buddha's twelve vows. Each general leads seven thousand yakshas, totaling eighty four thousand.",

      "id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-c01",
      "source_id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-s1",
      "quote": "In East Asian Buddhism, the Twelve Heavenly Generals or Twelve Divine Generals are the protective deities, or yaksha, of Bhaisajyaguru, the buddha of healing.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",

    {

      "id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-c03",
      "source_id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-s1",
      "quote": "The precise names of the generals seem to vary depending on tradition. Those listed below are from an available Sanskrit transcription of the Bhaiṣajyaguruvaiḍūryaprabhārāja Sūtra:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",

      "source_id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-s1",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemetaan zodiak dan honji juga bervariasi menurut teks dan daerah.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-c05",
      "source_id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-s2",
      "quote": "十二神将は、薬師如来の十二の大願に応じて、それぞれが昼夜の十二の時、十二の月、または十二の方角を守るという。そのため中国や日本では十二支が配当された。また、十二神将にはそれぞれ本地（化身前の本来の姿）の如来・菩薩・明王がある。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      }

      "source_id": "twelve-heavenly-generals-s2",
      "quote": "各神将がそれぞれ7千、総計8万4千の眷属夜叉を率い、それは人間の持つ煩悩の数に対応しているという。",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setiap jenderal memimpin tujuh ribu yaksha, total delapan puluh empat ribu.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Uchek Langmeitong adalah kisah Meitei tentang Hayainu yang menjadi rangkong karena perlakuan buruk ibu tirinya.",
    "en": "Uchek Langmeitong is a Meitei tale of Hayainu becoming a hornbill because of her stepmother's mistreatment.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "uchek-langmeitong-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Uchek Langmeitong adalah kisah Meitei tentang Hayainu yang menjadi rangkong karena perlakuan buruk ibu tirinya. Hayainu juga disebut Nongdam Atombi.",
      "en": "Uchek Langmeitong is a Meitei tale of Hayainu becoming a hornbill because of her stepmother's mistreatment. Hayainu is also called Nongdam Atombi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "uchek-langmeitong-c01",
        "uchek-langmeitong-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ayahnya pergi mencari penghasilan dan meninggalkannya bersama ibu tiri yang memaksanya bekerja serta memukulinya. Versi Funga Wari Singbul Sharma menyebut gadis Nongdangnu yang berubah menjadi burung dan menunggu ayahnya di pohon.",
      "en": "Her father leaves to earn money, leaving her with a stepmother who forces her to work and beats her. Her dead mother appears in a dream and suggests collecting hornbill feathers.
